decisions:
  - key: "reliability: dropping a timed-out client joins its runtime thread, so a slow connect-time lookup can hold a fetch past FETCH_TIMEOUT — `runtime/src/primitives/fetch_archive.rs`"
    outcome: waived
    reason: the GaiResolver it replaced behaves the same way, and in production the lookup is getaddrinfo, which the operating system resolver's own timeouts bound
    decided-at: 2026-10-20T04:21:02Z
    decided-by: andy@stone.dev
  - key: "reliability: the initial and redirect-screen lookups run outside the client's timeout — `runtime/src/primitives/fetch_archive.rs`"
    outcome: waived
    reason: unchanged by this fix and bounded the same way, by the operating system resolver's timeouts; FETCH_DEADLINE bounds the download itself
    decided-at: 2026-10-20T04:21:02Z
    decided-by: andy@stone.dev
  - key: "security: a redirect hop is looked up twice rather than connected to the address its screen resolved — `runtime/src/primitives/fetch_archive.rs`"
    outcome: waived
    reason: 022's scenario allows an equivalent connect-time re-check, both lookups are screened, and the owner prescribed this resolver and its staged-rebind test
    decided-at: 2026-10-20T04:21:02Z
    decided-by: andy@stone.dev
  - key: "other: two proxy tests each define the same four-line internal-address lookup — `runtime/src/primitives/fetch_archive.rs`"
    outcome: waived
    reason: test fixtures local to each test; sharing one would couple the tests for no gain
    decided-at: 2026-10-20T04:21:02Z
    decided-by: andy@stone.dev
  - key: "other: 025's ticked task 2 lists a malformed warning among the step-5 outcomes that task 10 replaced — `specs/025-rule-opt-out/tasks.md`"

    reason: "spent task history: each records what the task said when it was done, and rewriting it records a fiction"

    decided-by: andy@stone.dev
  - key: "other: [rules] surfaces is validated only when a rule directory exists, while disabled entries are validated always — `runtime/src/primitives/discover_rule_files.rs`"

# Review — 022-deterministic-runtime

## Summary

022's review of the window its record's diff-base starts, 282 scope entries, derived by compute-review-scope. Method: a first run of six partial reviewers (1,848,341 tokens) stopped with most of their 1.0 to 1.3 MB parts unread, and only their verified items are kept; then a pilot and 22 parts of about 550 KB each (8,910,447 tokens for the 23, mean 387,410), each reading all nine backend rule files whole and running all five passes over its slice. 156 entries were read whole by a part. Unread: 72 large files read by the hunks of every window commit and the functions containing them; 30 generated copies, whose generators were re-run and reported in sync; framework/bootstrap/govern.md, byte-identical to ductus.md by cmp; and the paths the fix rounds change, which the rounds re-read as diffs. The merged hand-backs were deduplicated (F3 is F19; O106, O112 and O117 are O100; O61 is O134; O26, O48 and O118 are O42; O55 is O49; O58 and O69 are O6). The 24 findings and 44 observations fixed are 022 tasks 41 to 46 and 48, under the owner's decisions of 2026-11-17: the containment and create-race classes are closed (task 46 audits what the 66-row grep missed, and task 48 contains the spec-root listing), Claude's four canonical allows are retired, rewrite-spec-links holds from to the feature-directory rule (051 amended and closed again), run-generator is host-run, dashboard keeps its whole refusal (026's audit containment test follows), and resolve_markdownlint is not a project-file read. Task 47 fixed a Unix-only test that runtime.yml's Windows job failed at aeee5ae9. The other 87 observations are waived by the rule with their reasons. The fix-round loop ran from start-fix-rounds' base 050ef7a6, budget 2, over 13 rounds: re-reads found a SHOULD at rounds 1, 3 and 7, each fixed by its prescribed edit and diff-checked; the owner let the loop go on past the budget (2026-11-17); every later re-read was clean, two re-read NOTEs were fixed, and the last round, the tasks' ticks, was exempt. docs/developing-ductus.md's change (2f505919) was read by round 1.

13 fix round(s): 9 re-read, 3 diff-checked, 1 exempt, 0 not re-read. Exempt: round 13.

## MUST violations (blocking)

*None.*

## SHOULD violations (advisory)

*None.*

## Low-confidence findings

*None.*

## Waived findings

*None.*

## Deferred findings

*None.*

## Observations

- security — extract-archive writes into the project when its dest is an ancestor of the working directory inside a staging root. validate_staging_outside refuses a dest that resolves inside the working directory but not one that contains it, so with the project under a staging root (a checkout under /tmp on Linux, or a TMPDIR override) extract-archive --dest <root>/x for a project at <root>/x/proj and an archive holding proj/.claude/settings.local.json wrote the project's own settings file. Probed on the release binary with TMPDIR set to a fixture directory: count 4, the file landed in the project. Plan section Agent reach says the destination must lie strictly inside a staging root and outside the working directory so a project inside a staging root is not written with no prompt, which is the case this leaves open; the same route reaches .ductus/bin/ductus, which task 22 closes for other writers. fetch-archive's file output cannot contain the project, so only extract-archive is reached. — `runtime/src/primitives/mod.rs:2239-2255` (breaks the spec's contract) — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- security — append-inbox creates a missing inbox by copying framework/templates/project/inbox.md read through any symlink, so a committed link there would copy an outside file into the tracked inbox; the path is a literal, the template exists only in the framework source repository, and the destination is contained. — `runtime/src/primitives/append_inbox.rs:82` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- security — create-feature and create-plan-artifacts read their template through resolve_template, which probes framework/templates/spec/ and the spec root's templates directory with no containment, so a template that is a symbolic link leaving the project (a tracked link in a cloned repository, since the candidate names are fixed: spec.md, plan.md, tasks.md, data-model.md) is read and copied into the new artifact. The feature directory is contained and walked; the template location is not. — `runtime/src/primitives/mod.rs:4294` — **waived**: the read reaches only a file of one of four fixed names and its content lands in a file in the project the operator is already creating; resolve_template is in mod.rs, outside this part, and the plan's Path arguments names path arguments and committed-text paths, which a fixed template name is not (decided by the rule)
- security — the .gitignore layer of the writeCode secret guard answers 'not ignored' when libgit2 errors (is_gitignored unwrap_or(false)) and is skipped when the project sits in no readable work tree, so a check that could not run looks like one that passed; secret_pattern on the file's basename and its canonical target stays the floor — `runtime/src/interpreter/payload.rs:1964` — **waived**: the degradation is stated on is_gitignored and on load_plan_relevant_files (gitignore is an opt-in second layer, the secret-pattern check the floor) and writecode-payload-bundling requires no failure mode for the layer; the guard only refuses, so a skipped layer sends no more than the plan's own listed files inside the project (decided by the rule)
- security — low confidence. Host::load_host_block reads the committed .ductus/config.toml with no link check, while the gitignored session file beside it is now judged by session::contained; a committed config that is a link leaving the project would be read, and toml's parse error quotes the offending line, so a line of a file outside the project could reach the error text. The plan section Path arguments names no class that holds this fixed path, and every primitive that loads config reads it the same way, so the gap is the runtime's, not Host::load's alone. — `runtime/src/host.rs:130` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- security — ductus parse FILE reads any path it is given with no containment and prints the parsed procedure (the whole AST, content included) when the file is Procedure-shaped markdown; every primitive path argument goes through validate_contained. The subcommand is CLI-only (excluded from the MCP registry by NON_PRIMITIVE_SUBCOMMANDS) and is driven by scripts/lint-procedure-parseability.sh over framework/commands. — `runtime/src/main.rs:635-681` — **waived**: a local CLI debug and CI surface the MCP server does not expose, supplied by the operator or a script, and an agent that can run it can already read the same file with its shell; no host-to-runtime trust boundary lies between the path's supplier and its reader, and the plan's path-argument section scopes the check to primitives (decided by the rule)
- security — the seeded Bash(.ductus/bin/ductus enforce-manifest *) deletes any file matching its glob inside the project with no prompt (checked on a fixture: a directory under the project lost its markdown file; a directory outside the project was refused for its parent-directory component); plan section Agent reach decides only run-another-command, write-outside-project and host-settings routes — `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md:64` — **waived**: Step 8 runs it, it is bounded to the project by the traversal check and the protected-destination screens, and a deletion inside a git work tree is recoverable; the plan's three categories do not include project-internal writes. (decided by the rule)
- security — Pointer materialization steps 3 and 4 put the Branch 1 [runtime] path into the ln -sf and cp command strings with no quoting or character rule, where {pin} and the ref get an explicit grammar for the same reason (later steps interpolate them); Branch 1 step 3 already executes that path with --version, and the seed grants neither command, so each prompts on Claude — `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md:461` — **waived**: The committed path already names a binary this run executes (048's stated escape hatch for a project-supplied runtime), so a quoting rule adds no boundary the executing step does not already cross. (decided by the rule)
- security — the framework archive, whose files include .githooks/pre-commit that runs on every commit, is fetched with no digest check (fetch-archive returns verified false without a sidecar, and --ref=main names a moving ref), where the runtime binary is held to a missing-sidecar-is-a-failure rule — `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md:32` — **waived**: Recorded design of 015, 048 and 061: GitHub's source tarballs carry no sidecar, the ref is chosen by the operator, and the fetch is HTTPS-only with TLS 1.2 or later; no commit in this window changed it. (decided by the rule)
- security — ductus-procedure.md Hook Installation case 5 and the manual snippet interpolate H and P (from git rev-parse --show-prefix, the project's directory path) into git config core.hooksPath {H} and ./{P}.githooks/ductus-pre-commit without quoting; a subdirectory project whose path holds a space or shell metacharacter would split or alter the command the agent runs (BE-INPUT-003's vector-argument requirement, applied as in 064's quoting fix for the release URL). At the work tree root H is the constant .githooks. — `framework/bootstrap/ductus-procedure.md:254` — **waived**: BE-INPUT-003 does not fire against a markdown step an agent renders, and the path is the operator's own project directory, not caller input. Quoting only the non-empty-P case is a design choice, since the seed's exact rule for git config core.hooksPath .githooks matches the unquoted constant and a blanket quote would make every root project prompt; it is not a mechanical edit. (decided by the rule)
- convention — check_no_protected decides which refusal to swallow by matching the refusal's message text (the words configuration directory followed by a backtick), so rewording refuse() would change what merge-permissions may write; a_config_dir_settings_file_stays_allowed and a_protected_path_is_refused hold both directions, so such a drift fails a test rather than passing. — `runtime/src/primitives/host_config.rs:375-390` — **waived**: Both directions are pinned by tests that fail on a reworded refusal, and the coupling is stated in the function's comment; a typed refusal variant would be a design change to a shared surface rather than a mechanical chore. (decided by the rule)
- other — create-feature, given two different names under one branch-scoped identifier at the same moment, lets both create their directory with the same n, which the code comment states; the number allocation is a check-then-act that claim_feature_dir covers only for one name. The design and its record belong to spec 051, so a fix lands in specs/051-branch-scoped-spec-numbering. — `runtime/src/primitives/create_feature.rs:58-82` — **waived**: The behavior is stated in the code and in 051's own plan, and it changes 051's contract, not 022's; it is another spec's to decide. (decided by the rule)
- convention — create-scenario writes through write_new_atomic, which leaves a new file at the tempfile's 0600 mode and re-applies none, where create-feature mirrors the template's mode; write_atomic gave the same mode before task 33, so the window did not change it, but a scenario lands more private than its siblings in a shared checkout. — `runtime/src/primitives/create_scenario.rs:47-52` — **waived**: Unchanged by the window (the previous write_atomic gave the same mode), git records only the executable bit, and no 022 criterion states a mode for a scenario file. (decided by the rule)
- other — reapply_source_mode returns true both when it chmodded and when it left a symbolic-link destination alone, so a link destination whose mode was never re-applied is not named in modes-kept while its entry reads unchanged; the doc comment states the link is deliberately left alone because the bytes compared were its target's. — `runtime/src/primitives/apply_manifest.rs:484-509` — **waived**: The doc comment and the test an_unchanged_destination_that_is_a_link_is_not_chmodded_through state it as the design: the mode is a property of the file the link names, which the destination check did not judge. (decided by the rule)
- other — raw_writes_allowlist counts only calls spelled write_atomic or write_atomic_bytes before the trailing test module, so a rewrite through std::fs::write, File::create, an aliased import or code after a test module is not seen; its doc comment names the first three limits, and line_ending_discipline counts guarded writers from the other side. — `runtime/src/primitives/guarded.rs:100-180` — **waived**: The limits are stated in the module doc, a grep of non-test code found no std::fs::write or File::create rewriting a project file read first, and the check is a review aid, not a claim of proof. (decided by the rule)
- bug — writeSpecBody and routeInboxItem requests are unbounded, against data-model §Request bodies past the read size (a request is held to read-size-bytes, legacy tail included) and task 37's done-when; the table in that section omits both points — `runtime/src/interpreter/payload.rs:792` (breaks the spec's contract) — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- bug — locate_exec_command labels every Host::contain_candidate error command-file-outside-project, including the Io error for a project root that cannot be resolved, where locate_command_file maps only InvalidPath to the outside-project cause and returns other errors as errors — `runtime/src/main.rs:832` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — main.rs error paths use eprintln!, which panics when stderr is a pipe whose reader has gone, so a failing run can exit 101 instead of 1; the closed-stdout scenario scopes itself to stdout and names the eprintln sites as unchanged — `runtime/src/main.rs:455` — **waived**: a-closed-stdout-is-a-quiet-exit scopes its behavior to the stdout result writers and states main.rs still writes errors with eprintln, and the run is already failing when these fire (decided by the rule)
- other — the CLI flag hydration is a table of 21 hydrate_field calls and tests prove only the shared helper and two primitives, so a call dropped or pointed at the wrong argument (applied against expired waivers, say) fails no test; every_mcp_argument_has_a_cli_form pins clap presence only — `runtime/src/main.rs:339` — **waived**: d3192610 records the hydration helper proved red with its assignment removed, and each of the 21 calls pairs a flag with its same-named field, so a swap shows in review as a name mismatch (decided by the rule)
- other — load_plan_relevant_files reads specs/{feature}/plan.md with a feature taken from the walker context and no validate_feature_argument, so a feature of ../x opens a plan outside the spec root — `runtime/src/interpreter/payload.rs:1528` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — AC28 in spec.md lists exec, mcp, parse and help as the names excluded from the set-equality, while NON_PRIMITIVE_SUBCOMMANDS in main.rs also excludes hook (spec 080), each with its reason in the test's doc comment — `specs/022-deterministic-runtime/spec.md:248` — **waived**: the enumeration sits in 022's spec.md, which reviewer F reads, and the code and its doc comment state all five exclusions with their reasons (decided by the rule)
- perf — each assessSpecQuality request reads and canonicalizes the spec once and runs the read-size check twice (fits in the builder, then again in bound_lists), each of which reads and parses the project config, so a 192-rule walk does about 400 config parses — `runtime/src/interpreter/payload.rs:1329` — **waived**: each parse is a sub-millisecond read of one small file and the walk is bounded at 192 requests, each of which waits on a host round trip that costs seconds (decided by the rule)
- other — session-file tier resolution (newest_existing) probes with exists(), which follows links, so a dangling link at the newest tier reads as absent and the next older tier is chosen — `runtime/src/schema/paths.rs:158` — **waived**: a link leaving the project is refused by session::contained before any open, and a dangling link at the newest tier is the contributor's own repair to make; no rule or contract of 022 names the case (decided by the rule)
- other — runtime/CHANGELOG.md says the walker sends 'one request per assessed rule, up to 192 per walk', and 022's data-model §assessSpecQuality repeats it; 192 is spec 060's count of rule sections with a Verification paragraph in 11 files on 2026-09-26, not a cap the code applies and not the walk's maximum. The walker asks once per loaded rule of the step's tier, and the 12 shipped rule files now hold 310 sections, 224 of them MUST-tier (counted 2026-11-16 over framework/rules/*.md Statements), so a full-stack walk's MUST step can send more than 192. — `runtime/CHANGELOG.md:446` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — scripts/mutation/session-file-containment.py records mutation M13 (the ductus exec seed in runtime/src/main.rs) as 'proved by hand with cargo test --bin ductus' because run.py runs only --lib and --test filters. The killing test exists (main.rs an_exec_seed_refuses_a_shared_default_linked_outside_the_project, line 1820) and the call it guards is at main.rs:775, but the proof cannot be re-run, so a later edit that drops that contained() call is caught by the test alone, with nothing showing the test fails without it. — `scripts/mutation/session-file-containment.py:1-12` — **waived**: The gap is stated in the list's own header and the killing test is in the tree; extending run.py to bin tests is new harness work, an observation about the pipeline's own machinery made while reviewing framework work, which AGENTS.md Workflow waives with its reason. (decided by the rule)
- other — scripts/mutation/command-file-containment.py (M6-M10 are unix-only) and scripts/mutation/installed-file-modes.py (M7 and M8 macOS-only, M5 a 50 ms ctime wait) leave EXPECTED_SURVIVORS empty, so run.py exits non-zero on a platform where those link or chflags tests do not run; EXPECTED_SURVIVORS is defined as the survivors no portable test can kill, and these are platform-bound. Each list's docstring states the bound. — `scripts/mutation/command-file-containment.py:22-27` — **waived**: The bound is stated in each docstring and the lists run on the maintainer's macOS tree, where all mutants are killed (10 of 10 per the commit); the harness's platform handling is the pipeline's own machinery. (decided by the rule)
- other — session-lock-backoff.py filters every mutant with the substring 'session::' and session-file-containment.py with 'session::tests::', which also match write_session and migrate_session_file tests, so a mutant counts as killed when any matching test fails, not necessarily the named killing tests the docstring lists. — `scripts/mutation/session-lock-backoff.py:30-90` — **waived**: Every mutant here edits runtime/src/session.rs, whose own tests are the ones the docstring names; the broader substring only widens the net, and the list's purpose, proving some test fails, holds. Harness machinery. (decided by the rule)
- other — runtime/tests/line_ending_discipline.rs without_test_module cuts a file at its first '#[cfg(test)]' of any kind, so a '#[cfg(test)]' helper placed mid-file (bounded_lists.rs line 225, rule_verdicts.rs line 41 today) hides every writer call after it from the scan; no scanned file has a writer after such a helper now, and the corpus-size guards would not notice one. — `runtime/tests/line_ending_discipline.rs:126-131` — **waived**: Latent: checked 2026-11-16, no primitives file holds a text-writer call after a non-module '#[cfg(test)]' item. The file's header already states the lint is per file and not per function. (decided by the rule)
- other — pass() and fail() are copied across the scripts/tests/*.sh files: 18 of them define '^pass() ' now, and test-host-namespace-parity.sh, added in this window, is one more copy. — `scripts/tests/test-host-namespace-parity.sh:24-26` — **waived**: There is no shared test library to reuse, and adding one for two lines is not this change's work.
- other — scripts/check-runtime-currency.sh accepts 'npm install -g name@1.2.3' as a pin (task: 347d985b), and the script's comment only requires an exact version; unlike cargo install, whose crate cargo verifies against the registry checksum, npm's exact version carries no checksum the build verifies, which SEC-DEPS-001 lists as the immutable-reference form for a downloaded tool. Its one user is windows-agent-session.yml (outside this manifest), installing @anthropic-ai/claude-code@2.1.325 for a measurement job. — `scripts/check-runtime-currency.sh:324-336` — **waived**: The subject is a check script reading a workflow another reviewer holds; an exact npm version is the immutable-by-registry form this script already accepts for cargo and pip, and whether the agent-session workflow's install meets SEC-DEPS-001 belongs to the review of that workflow. (decided by the rule)
- convention — configuration-cross.md CFG-ENV-006's Verification, as 74f6361c rewrote it, yields any other internal detail in a production error response to security-backend.md BE-ERR-001 with no load condition, though configuration-cross.md is a -cross file and 008's data-model Verification phrasing item 6, added in that same commit, requires a yield from a -cross rule to a -backend owner to read where that file is also loaded; in a project that does not load security-backend.md nobody keeps the case. - bug — merge-permissions' protected-set check ends at the first config-directory refusal, so a link under a config directory reaches protected paths. check_no_protected delegates to check_destination and swallows its refusal by matching the reason text 'configuration directory' followed by a backtick, but check_destination returns on its first refusal, so the as-given judgment of a path such as .claude/sub/pre-commit (sub a link to ../.git/hooks) ends the walk and the landing, links-lead and outer work-tree judgments never run. Probed on the release binary in a fixture: merge-permissions --path .claude/sub/pre-commit --allow Edit created .git/hooks/pre-commit (mode 0600, the permission JSON), while gitlink/hooks/pre-commit2 through a plain link to .git was refused as task 20 says. Plan section Agent reach says merge-permissions is judged through check_no_protected, the protected set and the MCP and settings files, by the three judgments. The control flow also depends on a message string, which an edit to refuse() would change silently. — `runtime/src/primitives/host_config.rs:375-390` (breaks the spec's contract) — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- bug — the raw-write allowlist test cannot see production code after a mid-file test module in primitives/mod.rs. raw_write_calls cuts each file at the first cfg(test) module, but mod.rs holds a small cfg(test) module (project_artifact_tests, line 3926) followed by about 430 lines of production code (tracked_project_paths to is_cell_path, lines 3986 to 4416) and the big tests module only at 4417, so a raw write_atomic call added there is neither counted nor flagged, while the test still reports the file clean at its count of 2. Probed by re-running the scan's regex over all 117 source files: mod.rs is the only file with production items after its first test module. - other — the scenario slug ductus-bootstrap is cited as a spec 022 scenario in seven live places, but the scenario file is govern-bootstrap.md (title Ductus bootstrap); no file named ductus-bootstrap ever existed in git history. 022's plan.md line 338 links it correctly with the label ductus-bootstrap and the path govern-bootstrap.md. Citations outside this part's table: runtime/src/main.rs:866, runtime/src/primitives/fetch_archive.rs:5, scripts/lint-procedure-parseability.sh:78, specs/022-deterministic-runtime/plan.md:338, scenarios/apply-manifest.md:9, scenarios/runtime-primitive-structural-bugs.md:13, runtime/tests/fixtures/ductus-basic/framework/bootstrap/install.md:28. — `specs/022-deterministic-runtime/scenarios/govern-bootstrap.md:1` — **waived**: Not mechanical: the repair is a choice between renaming the scenario file to ductus-bootstrap.md (one rename and one plan.md link; the digest key in analysis.md regenerates) and re-pointing seven prose citations in four directories, including a runtime/ comment batch. The scenario is reachable by its link in plan.md and its title, so no reader is sent to a missing file by a link; it holds no requirement. (decided by the rule)
- bug — writecode-payload-bundling Behavior 3 gives the WriteCodeRequest field order as constitution-excerpts, plan-relevant-files, write-boundary, task, but the struct in runtime/src/schema/extensions.rs also carries constitution-excerpts-unexaminable between the first two (omitted from the wire when empty, part of the stable prefix). The enumeration the scenario states as the contract is incomplete against the tree; the cache-anchor position it requires (after write-boundary, before task) is unaffected. — `specs/022-deterministic-runtime/scenarios/writecode-payload-bundling.md:19` (breaks the spec's contract) — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — two Resolved Questions in this part say a real adopter bootstrap verifies or is needed for a change (an-unsubstituted-placeholder-is-not-a-clean-run line 32, every-mcp-argument-has-a-cli-form line 31), while AGENTS.md Workflow now says no real adopter run exists and the scratch-adopter bootstrap stands in (owner, 2026-11-16); 6464d1a9, in the window and naming 022, made that edit in 022's tasks.md and left the two scenarios. — `specs/022-deterministic-runtime/scenarios/an-unsubstituted-placeholder-is-not-a-clean-run.md:32` — **waived**: Each is a decision record in the owner's 2026-11-12 words, and a sweep keeps the record of the decision it sweeps (AGENTS.md Gotchas); the decision itself, to verify through a bootstrap, still holds, and the 2026-11-16 stand-in is recorded in AGENTS.md Workflow and 022's tasks.md. (decided by the rule)
